# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド35・36（レッスン 1021〜1080）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校・中学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 1021 --to 1080`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 37 / info 27 → あと error 0 / warn 27 / info 27**（のこりは ぜんぶ わざと: アマラの 犬の なまえ「Biscuit」9、テストで きまった タイトルの「…」と その スライド 10、L1078 ビリモの「very, very, very cute!」〈事実では ない 文の わるい 例〉6、L1056 の せつめい「A big white board」2。info は 会話の「What is / it is」・「very, very」〈オズウィンさんの ゆっくり〉・Let's 4回〈Let's pretend の ため〉）
- なおした 欄: **507**（パッチ 536 件。ブロックの きめごと 125・AI の さいしょの ひとこと 60・レビュアー 329・あとの そろえ 22）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で、HEAD から じゅんばんに あてなおした（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあい・かいわ あいて（partner.ts）・文法の じゅんばん（order.mjs。テストが 見ない teacher.segments も）を たしかめた（ALL OK）

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. イギリスふうの ほめことば: 「Brilliant!」→「Great job!」（L1022・L1029・L1033・L1036・L1042・L1051・L1068・L1076）、「Lovely!」→「Nice job! / Wonderful!」（L1029・L1036・L1047）
2. エマ先生 じぶんの 語り: say・字幕の「Ms. Emma says / asks」→「I say / I ask」（日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」）、teacherScript の「Ms. Emma says / holds / finds / whispers / puts / explains / gives ～」「with Ms. Emma」→ I / me / together（L1021〜1080 の 30 レッスン）。日本語の「先生が きくよ / いうよ」も「わたしが ～」に（L1031・L1033・L1034・L1038・L1039）。こどもが エマ先生に はなしかける 文と キコの 計画カードは なまえの まま
3. 日本語の 先生の なまえ「ミス・エマ / ミズ・エマ」→「エマ先生」（L1045・L1072・L1074）
4. エマ先生の あいさつ「Good morning, ～!」→「Hi / Hello, ～!」（L1022・L1033・L1043・L1062 の さいしょ）。L1066 の 発表の はじめかた「Good morning, everyone. Have you ever seen ～?」（学習項目）と TPR「Wave and say, Good morning!」は のこした
5. 「Or, ～」→「Or ～」（L1022・L1027）、okay → OK（L1054・L1057・L1059）
6. **AI の さいしょの ひとこと**: アプリは あいてを こどもの キャラ（ハル・リコ・キコ・ビリモ）から えらび、その かおと なまえで ひとことを 出す。なのに「I'm Olive.」「I'm Priya!」「I'm Mayor Hart.」「I'm Lumi」や、ヴェイル博士・オズウィンさん・ルミとして 話して いた。goalJa の やくは のこし、60 レッスン ぜんぶ「Let's pretend I'm Olive / Priya / Amara / Leo / Ken / Mayor Hart / the librarian / Lumi / Dr. Vale / Mr. Oswin.」に した（W25-34 と おなじ）。2つめの Let's は「Time to / We can」に、ルミの「Let's find out!」は ひとことでは とった。L1061 の 27 語の 文を みじかく、L1044 lovely → great
7. **アメリカ英語**: L1048「in the holidays」→「during vacation」（学習項目の 表現も）、L1023 ことば「timetable」→「schedule」、L1045「Tick!」→「Check!」
8. **温度は「degrees Celsius」**: アメリカの こどもは「4 degrees」を 華氏（こおる より つめたい）と きく。各レッスンの さいしょの 1回だけ「degrees Celsius」に（L1053・L1055・L1067・L1072・L1076）
9. **声に 出す ための なおし**: たんどくの 字の かたまり（ity・ble・bil・ous・tion・Re, c, ei, ve）を 字の 読みあげ（i, t, y）や「the ending」に、こま切れの 音節（bi, OL, o, gist）を ハイフンの 拍（Bi-OL-o-gist）に。話す 人が ない こたえ（「Can I interview you? Of course!」）に 話す 人を つけた（W35・W36 の 20 か所 いじょう）

## 学習項目が かわった もの（テスト・src の 文字列は かえて いない＝どこにも 書かれて いない）
- L1023 vocab[3]「timetable」→「schedule」（アメリカ英語）
- L1042 tpr[3]「Shake your hand and say, No straw, thank you!」→「Shake your head and say, ～」（hand だと あくしゅ）
- L1048 school.expressions[1]「～ who will water the plants in the holidays?」→「～ during vacation?」
- L1053 school.expressions[1]「It reaches well only the top 200 meters.」→「Bright sunlight reaches only the top 200 meters.」（語順の まちがい。うすい ひかりは 1,000 m まで とどくので Bright を つけた）
- L1058 school.expressions[2]「～ they go back down in the day.」→「～ during the day.」
- src/lib/curriculum/w36Phonics.ts の biome の definition「climate, plants and animals」→「climate and living things」（しんかいに しょくぶつは ない。テストは この 文を 見て いない）
- core 表現・フォニックスの ことば・タイトルは かえて いない

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・テストで きまって いる・あとで 決める）
- タイトルと スライドの「…」、会話の「…」、オズウィンさんの「Wel... come...」
- 「Biscuit」（アマラの 犬の なまえ）
- たんどくの 語根 spect・dict・struct・port・rupt・ject・Inter・Co（ならう 音そのもの。W33 と おなじ）。TTS で きいて たしかめたい
- W36 の「pause here」が お手本の 文の なかに ある こと（ブロック全体の かたち。声で 読まれるので、あとで「Pause after X.」の かたちに する ことを 考える）
- 文の あたまの 名詞・動詞で 強勢が かわる ことば（Transport. Project. Export.）

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L1021-1030 (world 35, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters, schedule). There are 39 patches. L1026 and L1027 have no change. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "on the one hand" first appears in L1025, "have a chance to" in L1027, and "the right to" / "responsible for" / "suggest that" / "compromise" do not appear in this group. Themes: (1) **Bare letter groups that TTS misreads**: "ity" (L1021-1025, L1030: "Add ity", "the part before ity", "Ity makes a noun"), "ble" / "bil" (L1023-1025, L1030) and "ous" / "os" (L1021). They are now spelled as letters ("i, t, y", "b, l, e to b, i, l"), the same as W33's "Add t, y". The JA subtitles show the letters as text, so they stay. "Inter" and "Co" are kept (W33 precedent "Inter means between"; pronounceable). (2) **Leftovers from the block-wide I-say swap**: L1025 "I hold out my right hand ... Then she holds out her left hand" (no clear "she"). L1028 "First, I'll tell you three important things" now sits inside Olive's quote, so it reads as Olive. (3) **Story contradiction**: in L1028 seg6, "In the story, ... Kiko says calmly, Stop it ... Riko stands next to Tom". In the picture book it is Sam, and Kiko and Riko are only practicing. (4) **Small fixes**: in L1023 JA, "十分" (it reads as じゅうぶん = enough) is now 10ぷん. Also L1023 "Make your own idea", L1025 "not-so-good", L1028 "The internet is networks connected...", L1029 "A small question can make a big friend" and "A costar is a star of a movie together with". Facts and arithmetic checked: the L1024 survey adds up (15/20 seats, 12 breaks, 8 + 12 = 20 uniforms, 17 phones, 2 homework, five questions), the L1027 ball plan (Mon/Wed younger, Tue/Thu older, Fri mixed) matches the conversation, all stress marks are right (e-QUAL-i-ty, di-VER-si-ty, cu-ri-OS-i-ty, per-son-AL-i-ty, cre-a-TIV-i-ty, sim-i-LAR-i-ty, ac-TIV-i-ty, a-BIL-i-ty, pos-si-BIL-i-ty, ma-JOR-i-ty, pop-u-LAR-i-ty, re-spon-si-BIL-i-ty), the -ity spelling rules are right, and inter- = between / co- = together are right. Safety in L1028 is right (never fight, never laugh along, tell an adult right away), and L1027 / L1029 use made-up names. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 213-229 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1021 The Hall of Wisdom
- A: Willowford, the round green roof, Olive with her bell and the seats in a circle are warm and natural. Kiko's "speaking with everyone looking at me makes me a little nervous" is a lovely honest line. In seg7 it is shortened to "But it makes me a little nervous", which is fine.
- B: "Children from many places meet here to talk" is clear, and the -ity rules (drop the e in diverse; ous -> os in curious) are right. The stresses are right.
- C: TTS will misread the bare "ity" in seg9 ("Add ity"), seg10 ("before ity") and the teacherScript ("Ity makes a noun", "before ity"). It will also misread "ous to os" in the teacherScript.
- Decision: spell "i, t, y" in seg9, seg10 and the teacherScript, and "o, u, s to o, s" in the teacherScript (B+C).

#### L1022 Should Students Wear Uniforms?
- A: Leo's and Priya's two views, and "We can think differently and still be friends", are natural. Olive's "Good morning, everyone!" is inside the story (keeper). In seg8, "smile and say, I see your point, but." ends on "but". That is fine for naming a frame (single flag).
- B: Fact vs opinion is taught clearly, with good examples (Leo's school has uniforms = fact; uniforms look cool = opinion).
- C: Bare "ity" in seg9 ("More ity words"), seg10 and the teacherScript.
- Decision: spell "i, t, y" (B+C).

#### L1023 Longer Breaks?
- A: Amara's ten-minute break and Riko's longer-lunch idea are realistic. "break time" is kept (brief: only one reviewer would want recess). In seg17, "Make your own idea." is not natural English.
- B: Opinion + reason + example and "I partly agree" = yes to one part are clear. The ble -> bil rule is right. The same seg17 flag as A. In JA, seg0 "休み時間は 十分だけ" and seg12 "あと 十分" use the kanji 十分, which reads as じゅうぶん (enough). That says the opposite of Amara's point, and the conversation already writes 10ぷん.
- C: Bare "ity" and "ble" / "bil" (read as "bill") in seg9, seg10 and the teacherScript.
- Decision: seg17 -> "Share your own idea." (A+B). seg0 / seg12 JA -> 10ぷん (objective JA). Letters spelled in seg9, seg10 and the teacherScript (B+C). Priya says "I partly agree" in the teacherScript story but Kiko says it in the conversation. Both tellings are fine on their own (single flag, kept).

#### L1024 School Rules Survey
- A: The anonymous survey ("nobody had to share private information"), Leo being one of the two homework fans and Kiko's "some people might feel left out" are good.
- B: The numbers all add up. Most = more than half, out of and only a few are explained well, and the quiz (17/20 = most) is right.
- C: Bare "ity" and "ble becomes bil" in seg9, seg10 and the teacherScript.
- Decision: spell the letters (B+C).

#### L1025 School Rules Discussion
- A: The hands-out "on the one hand / on the other hand" demo and the summary board are great. In the teacherScript, "I hold out my right hand and say ... Then she holds out her left hand and says" is left over from the block-wide swap ("she" was Ms. Emma).
- B: The two-sides frame is introduced here, first, as the order rule needs, and the advantage / disadvantage quiz is right. In seg4, "A not so good point" before a noun needs hyphens.
- C: Same "she" flag (the voice cannot tell who "she" is). Bare "ity" / "ble to bil" in seg9, seg10 and the teacherScript.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Then I hold out my left hand and say," (objective). seg4 -> "A not-so-good point." (objective). Letters spelled (B+C). The first leaf grows, as the test needs.

#### L1026 Fair or Not Fair?
- A: Amara's story about Nina by the fence is touching, and "Maybe nobody told them how Nina feels" models empathy. The teacherScript line "International means between nations, like our council" overstates a little (the council members come from towns, not clearly from countries). Single flag, kept.
- B: "It's not fair if ~" and "Maybe they don't know that ~" are clear. inter = between / each other and co = together are right. "don't call anyone bad" in seg8 is grammatical (single flag).
- C: Reads well. "Inter" and "Co" alone are pronounceable (W33 precedent).
- Decision: no change.

#### L1027 Everyone Should Have a Chance
- A: Leo's ball plan and Mixed Team Day are realistic, and the sign-up sheet concern is a nice touch. Birimo's "Nice try, everyone!" at the end of a discussion is a little odd but echoes the TPR cheer (single flag).
- B: "How about + -ing" vs "Why don't we + verb" is taught right, and the quiz (How about taking turns) is right. "have a chance to" starts here, as the order rule needs. The plan days match the conversation.
- C: "plus ing" is pronounceable. Reads well.
- Decision: no change.

#### L1028 Standing Up for a Friend
- A: The safety message is right (never fight, never laugh along, calm words, tell an adult right away). In the teacherScript, "Let's think about it together. First, I'll tell you three important things." now reads as Olive speaking, because the block-wide swap put Ms. Emma's line inside Olive's quote. The line "The internet is networks connected with each other" is ungrammatical.
- B: In seg6, "In the story, Mia teases Tom. Kiko says calmly, Stop it ... Riko stands next to Tom" contradicts the picture book, where Sam does both. Kiko and Riko are practicing (conversation lines 1-2). Same "internet" flag as A.
- C: The speaker of the "three important things" line is unclear to the voice.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Then I say, Here are three important things." (A+C). "The internet connects computers with each other." matches seg9 (A+B). seg6 -> "Kiko practices and says calmly, ... Riko says, Then I'll stand next to my friend. ..." with JA to match (objective, story).

#### L1029 Including Everyone
- A: Ken on the bench, "ask first and listen to the answer" and "tomorrow, I might want to finish my book" are natural and respectful. The poster's "A small question can make a big friend." sounds like a large friend.
- B: "Do you want to join us?" / "It's OK if you ~. Maybe next time!" are clear. The intermission / coauthor / costar meanings are right. The teacherScript "A costar is a star of a movie together with another star" is clumsy, while seg10 says "acts in a movie with another star". The same poster flag as A.
- C: Reads well. "No thank you is OK, too" in seg8 is fine read aloud.
- Decision: poster -> "A small question can start a big friendship." (A+B). costar -> "A costar acts in a movie together with another star." (A+B).

#### L1030 Kindness Campaign
- A: The campaign roles (Amara draws, Priya spells, Leo writes the slogan) and the Q&A are natural. "the four friends give their presentation" is a little vague, and Priya is in the group but also asks a question (single flag, kept). Haru's "It only takes a second!" is a nice pun on Second.
- B: First / Second / Finally and "Thank you for listening. Do you have any questions?" are clear. The review of inter / co / -ity is right. Conversation 8 lines (allowed). The second leaf grows.
- C: Bare "ble becomes bil, plus ity" in seg10 and the teacherScript.
- Decision: spell the letters (B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Bare suffix letters in W35 phonics**: "ity", "ble" / "bil", "ous" / "os" were said alone in say and teacherScript all through L1021-1025 and L1030. Expect the same in later reviews that recall -ity (L1035, L1040, L1045-1050), and also bare roots in L1041-1044 ("spect", "dict", "struct", "port", "rupt", "ject"). W33 kept "rupt" (pronounceable), but "ject" and "struct" alone are worth a look. Grep say / teacherScript for `\b(ity|ble|bil|ous|ject|struct|spect|dict)\b` and spell the letters, or put them in a real word.
- **"Inter" / "Co" alone**: I kept them (pronounceable, and W33 kept "Inter means between"). If the TTS reads "Co" as "company" or "C-O", change "Co means together" in L1026-1030 at once.
- **I-say swap leftovers**: watch for "I ... Then she ..." pairs (L1025) and "I'll tell you" landing inside a character's quote (L1028) elsewhere in W35-36.
- **Kanji 十分**: if other JA subtitles write minutes as 十分 / 二十分, use 10ぷん (十分 reads as じゅうぶん).
- Kept on purpose: "break time"; Olive's "Good morning, everyone!" (story); "I see your point, but." as a frame name; L1026 "like our council"; L1027 "Nice try, everyone!"; L1029 "No thank you is OK, too"; L1030 "the four friends"; Birimo's feminine endings (のよ) when he plays Olive / Amara; "Please tell me" in the L1030 starter (Olive, polite); all "Well done!" / "Thumbs up" / "Ding!" / "Every voice matters!".

### Run 9 review notes: L1031-1040 (world 35, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 62 patches, and every lesson has at least one. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected (one expression **ja** changed in L1040: 電話 -> スマホ). The grammar order is clean: "have the right to" starts in L1031, "responsible for" in L1032 (L1031 has only "responsibility / responsibilities"), "suggest that" in L1036 and "compromise" in L1038, and no new text adds them earlier. Themes: (1) **Bare letter chunks in the formal-word spellings that TTS misreads**: "Re, c, ei, ve" / "Re, qu, est" (L1036, L1040), "A, s-s, ist" and "Pro, vide" (L1037), "Re, qu, ire" and "Re, pair" (L1038), "Bi, c, y, c, le" (L1039). They are now spelled as letters (as the teacherScripts already do), given in the hyphen beat form (re-PAIR), or carried by a rhyme (provide rhymes with ride). Bare "ity" in the teacherScript (L1035, L1040) is now "i, t, y", and "Make CU strong" (L1035) is now "Make the second beat strong", because TTS may spell CU as "see you". (2) **Story and contrast logic**: in L1039 Leo counted the votes, but the AI Leo says he missed the vote. Priya counts now. In L1034 the contrast drill asked "In the morning?" before "Saturday AFTERNOON", so two words changed. It now asks "Saturday morning?", the same as the tip. In L1035, "we cleaned the park with me" was left by the I-swap. (3) **JA**: "ふくの" read as 服 (L1033), opaque ちょうしゅう (L1035), スカーフ vs マフラー for Mayor Hart's red scarf (L1036 vs L1040/L1050), and 電話 for a phone map (L1040). (4) **Small US/natural fixes**: tap your card (L1036), a small piece of paper and think of an idea (L1037), wants to play soccer / to draw (L1038), "I pick this one!" (L1039), and podium (L1040). The facts and arithmetic are right: the 1989 list of children's rights (UN CRC), 12 + 5 + 3 = 20 votes, twelve out of twenty is more than half, the tally with a line across for five, i before e after c (receive), bi = two and cycle = wheel, and ph = f. "We also suggest that the town put" correctly uses the bare verb. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 222-229 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1031 Children Have Rights
- A: Olive's bell, Leo's "So soccer is a right!" and the rights poster are warm and natural. In seg5, "Voice. Your sound, and your idea." is not how a teacher defines voice.
- B: The 1989 agreement (UN Convention on the Rights of the Child) is right. "have the right to + verb" is introduced here, and "responsible for" is absent, as the order rule needs. The contrast-stress drill (THIS / THURSDAY / FOUR / RIGHT) changes one word each time. Same seg5 flag as A. seg17 "Which right is most important" without "the" is fine (single, kept).
- C: Reads well. The capitalized stress words are real words, so TTS reads them as words.
- Decision: seg5 -> "Voice. The sound you make, and your idea." (A+B).

#### L1032 We Are Responsible For…
- A: Biscuit, Pepper and the care chart are lovely. In seg8, "Yes, with one more idea? I think so, but..." is cryptic.
- B: The chart, the expressions and the conversation agree (Amara feeds Pepper in the evening and walks Biscuit with Dad in the morning). responsible for + -ing is right. The safety point (an adult on busy roads, go to the vet with a grown-up) is good.
- C: Same seg8 flag: it is choppy, and the question has no verb.
- Decision: seg8 -> "Agree, but want to add something? Say, I think so, but..." (A+C).

#### L1033 Helping at Home
- A: Priya's family chart and "a family is a team" are natural. Answering "Should children help at home every day?" with "I partly agree" is a little loose, since you agree with statements, but it is a learning item and is common in class (single area, kept).
- B: responsible for + -ing and the A/B quiz are right. In the seg9 JA, the answer to "Do you wash the dishes? No, I DRY the dishes" is just 「ううん、ふくの！」, which in hiragana reads as 服 (clothes).
- C: Reads well.
- Decision: seg9 subtitleJa -> 「ううん、おさらを ふくの！」 (objective JA).

#### L1034 Our Community
- A: The view from the Hall window and the clean-up notice (children must come with an adult) are good. In seg8, "Why don't we... is a friendly idea" calls the phrase itself an idea.
- B: The seg3 definition says neighbor = "lives next to you", but the JA says ちかくに. In seg9, "In the morning? No, it's on Saturday AFTERNOON!" changes two things, while the contrast rule (and the tip and teacherScript) asks "Saturday morning?".
- C: Same seg8 flag. In the seg9 drill, the voice cannot stress just one word when two are new.
- Decision: seg3 -> "lives near you" (objective, EN/JA). seg8 -> "a friendly way to share an idea" (A+C). seg9 -> "Saturday morning?" with JA 「土曜日の あさ？」 (B+C).

#### L1035 Rights and Responsibilities Talk
- A: Riko's speech and "Every voice matters, so every ear matters, too" are great. The teacherScript line "we cleaned Willowford Park with me and the other adults" comes from the block-wide swap and is self-contradictory. In seg6, "Riko stands up. I'd like to talk..." has no speaker tag.
- B: The third leaf is in the right place (L1035). The review words are right (interview, coexist, security = secure - e + ity). The teacherScript says "Say it strongly, se-CU-ri-ty", which stresses the whole word, not the beat before -ity as L1021-1024 taught. The seg3 JA ちょうしゅう is opaque for kids.
- C: In seg10, TTS may spell out "Make CU strong" as "C U" (see you). The teacherScript has a bare "add ity". Same seg6 flag (it sounds like Ms. Emma's own words).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "together with the other adults" (A+C) and "add i, t, y. Say the part before i, t, y strongly." (B+C). seg10 -> "Make the second beat strong." with JA 2つめの キュを つよく (B+C). seg3 JA -> きいて いる 人たち (objective JA). seg6 -> "She says, I'd like to talk..." (A+C).

#### L1036 I Suggest That We…
- A: Mayor Hart's formal letter (inform, receive, request) is the right target and stays. In seg5, "you touch your card here" is the タッチ calque. A US speaker taps a card.
- B: "I suggest that we + plain verb" is explained right, and "i before e after c" (r, e, c, e, i, v, e) is right. The seg0 JA calls the red scarf スカーフ, but L1040 (vocab, seg0, and seg5 "So warm!") and L1050 use マフラー.
- C: In seg10, "Re, c, ei, ve" / "Re, qu, est" are bare chunks that TTS misreads (ree / eye / vee / kyoo).
- Decision: seg5 -> "tap your card" (A+B). seg10 -> letter spelling, with JA to match (B+C). seg0 JA -> あかい マフラー (objective JA, consistency).

#### L1037 How About Making a Map?
- A: The brainstorm, sticky notes and Priya getting lost are natural. In seg17, "Make a new idea" is a calque. In seg4, "A small paper that sticks" treats uncountable paper as countable.
- B: How about + -ing is right, and the quiz is right. The formal pairs (buy/purchase, help/assist, give/provide) are real equivalents. Same seg17 flag as A.
- C: In seg9, "A, s-s, ist" and in seg10, "Pro, vide" are misread by TTS.
- Decision: seg4 -> "a small piece of paper" (objective). seg9 -> "It has a double s. A, s, s, i, s, t." (B+C). seg10 -> "It rhymes with ride. Provide." with JA ride と おなじ ひびき (B+C). seg17 -> "Think of a new idea" (A+B).

#### L1038 Let's Find a Compromise
- A: The empty lot, Leo vs Amara and the compromise are clear. The JA hint (だきょうあん) is allowed here. In seg17, "Your friend wants soccer, and you want art" is clipped.
- B: "I see your point, but" and "What if we" are right, and the compromise definition is right. "It will require some benches" is a slightly odd use, but it is the formal target word (kept).
- C: In seg9, "Re, qu, ire" and in seg10, "Re, pair" are bare chunks. Same seg17 flag.
- Decision: seg9 -> "R, e, q, u, i, r, e." (B+C). seg10 -> "re-PAIR." (B+C). seg17 -> "wants to play soccer, and you want to draw" (A+C). The JA already says おえかきが したい.

#### L1039 Vote and Report
- A: The ballot box, the tally and Kiko accepting the result are good. In seg2, "Me! This one!" does not fit choosing an idea.
- B: The arithmetic is consistent everywhere: 12 + 5 + 3 = 20, and twelve out of twenty is more than half, so it is the majority. The teacherScript says "Riko and Leo count the votes", but the AI Leo says "I was outside, so I missed the vote".
- C: In seg10, a bare "le" in "Bi, c, y, c, le". Same seg2 flag (choppy).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Riko and Priya count the votes" (objective, story; the starter frame is kept). seg2 -> "I pick this one!" with JA これに する！ (A+C). seg10 -> "B, i, c, y, c, l, e." (B+C).

#### L1040 Children's Proposal
- A: The podium, the applause and the mayor's yes make a nice finale. The fourth leaf is here, and the conversation has 8 lines, which is allowed. In seg3, "A stand where a speaker stands" is clumsy.
- B: "Our proposal is to ~. It will help ~" and "We also suggest that the town put" (bare verb) are right. The JA uses 電話 for "phone" six times. A Japanese child reads that as a telephone, which makes no sense in place of a map. The US "phone" here means a smartphone (スマホ).
- C: In seg9, "Re, c, ei, ve" is a bare chunk again. The teacherScript has a bare "Real plus ity". Same seg3 flag.
- Decision: 電話 -> スマホ in expressions[2].ja, conversation lines 3-4 ja, and seg7, seg16, seg18 JA (objective JA). seg3 -> "The place where a speaker stands." (A+C). seg9 -> letter spelling (B+C). teacherScript -> "Real plus i, t, y." (B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Bare "ity" everywhere in W35 say and teacherScript**: L1021-1030 and L1041-1050 say "add ity", "before ity", "plus ity" many times (and the mixed form "Popu-LAR-ity" in the L1046-1050 reviews). TTS reads "ity" as "itty" or spells it out. Spell it "i, t, y" (the teacherScripts of some lessons already do) or say "the ending". Grep `\bity\b` in say/subtitleEn/teacherScript.
- **Two-letter capital beats in "Make X strong"**: "CU" (L1035) is likely spelled out. "AL" (L1040), "LAR", "QUAL", "PUR" and "HAPS" are pronounceable, and I kept them. Check other short caps like "CU", "BIL" and "TIV" if a reviewer hears them spelled out.
- **Chunk spellings for formal words / roots**: if L1041-1050 spell roots in chunks ("spect", "dict", "struct", "rupt", "ject"), TTS may misread them. Use letters or real words.
- **"Co means together"** is used across L1026-1035. TTS may read capital "Co" as "company" on some voices. I kept it for consistency with the other groups (single flag).
- **JA 先生が きくよ / 先生が いうよ** for Ms. Emma's own "I ask / I say" (L1031, 1033, 1034, 1038, 1039) is natural teacher Japanese, so I kept it. The block-wide swap used わたし「 in other lessons, so the owner may want one form.
- **電話 for phone**: check L1041-1080 JA for 電話 where the EN means a smartphone (maps, searching, photos).
- Kept on purpose: "I partly agree" as an answer to yes/no questions (learning items); "Biscuit"; Birimo's feminine endings when he plays Olive or Mayor Hart; "Wave and say, Good morning!" (L1034 TPR); "This morning" / "tomorrow" in the story; JA "イエス！" for the mayor's yes (matches the TPR); the titles with "…".

### Run 9 review notes: L1041-1050 (world 35, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters, during vacation, schedule). There are 58 patches, and every lesson has at least one. One learning item changed: the L1042 TPR "Shake your hand and say, No straw, thank you!" is now "Shake your head and say, ..." (tpr[3].en / ja / action, seg12 say / subtitles, the seg12 and seg13 slides). No test or src line references it (grep of tests/ and src/lib/curriculum/ for "Shake your hand" is empty). The expressions, vocab and phonics words are untouched. The grammar order is clean: no new text adds on the one hand / chance to / right to / responsible for / suggest that / compromise anywhere new. The core expressions, the regex cores, the story words (leaf, map, sea), "Now, phonics." and the ほんとうの goalJa (L1047) are kept. Themes: (1) **Story numbers**: in L1042 Priya counted 31 bottles "yesterday" ("Wow, in only one day!"), but in L1044 the whole council used only 120 in a month. It is now "this week" (31 a week, about 120 a month). (2) **Answers with no speaker**: L1041 seg8 ("It's a fact"), L1045 seg6 ("Not at all!") and seg7 ("Sure, no problem") sounded like the asker answering. They now name the speaker, as in L961. (3) **Bare letter groups that TTS misreads**: "ity" (L1046, L1047, L1049), "Ble / bil" (L1048 teacherScript) and "ous / os" (L1049). They are now spelled as letters, the way the L1048 seg9 already did. "Popu-LAR-ity" is now the full beat form "Pop-u-LAR-i-ty". (4) **Small fixes**: L1041 "phonics.Spect" (missing space), L1041 "I think yes, because" became "I think they should, because", L1043 "we suggested our own bottles", L1044 the heteronym project (verb), L1045 Riko vs Kiko in the teacherScript, British "Tick!", L1046 "a short writing", L1047 the signature definition and JA アドレス, L1050 two "Next" in a row. Facts checked are right: 120 -> 40 is two thirds less, forty chairs, four benches everywhere (letter, compromise, L1050 news), wild animals and sanctuaries are presented safely (visit with an adult or family), and five then six golden leaves. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 207-229 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1041 Animals and Us
- A: The baby fox wish, Leo's "a fox is a wild animal" and Amara's sanctuary idea make a warm, safe topic. Birimo (as Amara) says "I think yes, because baby foxes are so cute!", which is not how a US child answers "Should people ~?". In seg8 the answer "It's a fact." has no speaker after "Kiko asks".
- B: On the one hand / On the other hand starts at L1025, so it is fine here. The roots (spect = look, dict = say, struct = build, pre = before, in = inside) are right. "I think they should" models should + base verb better than "I think yes". The quiz answer slide (seg19) shows the "It's a fact" expression although the answer is "an opinion". It only shows the learning line, so I kept it (single flag). teacherScript "phonics.Spect" has no space.
- C: seg8 has the same speaker flag as A. "phonics.Spect" runs together. "Spect", "Dict" and "Struct" said alone are easy to pronounce.
- Decision: "I think yes, because" -> "I think they should, because" in the conversation, seg6 say / subtitleEn and the seg6 slide (A+B). seg8 -> "I answer, It's a fact." with JA わたし「じじつよ。…」 (A+C). teacherScript "phonics.Spect" -> "phonics. Spect" (objective).

#### L1042 Less Plastic
- A: Priya's blue box, the poster and the bottle sticker are natural. The TPR "Shake your hand and say, No straw, thank you!" is not idiomatic (it sounds like a handshake). A US child shakes the head.
- B: I suggest that we + base verb, How about + -ing and Why don't we + verb are all modeled right. port = carry, rupt = break, ject = throw, ex = out, inter = between and re = back are right. Story numbers: "Yesterday ... thirty-one! Wow, in only one day!" does not fit L1044, where the council used 120 bottles in the whole month. The plastic facts (hundreds of years to break down, it hurts turtles and seabirds) are right.
- C: Reads well. "Glug, glug!" and "Slurp!" are fun.
- Decision: "yesterday" -> "this week" in the teacherScript (with "Wow, in only one week!"), conversation line 0 (JA こんしゅう), seg6, the seg6 and seg18 slides and the starter (objective, story numbers). TPR "Shake your hand" -> "Shake your head" in tpr[3] (en / ja / action), seg12 and the seg12 / seg13 slides (A+B, learning item, no test reference).

#### L1043 Stage 4 Review: Opinions
- A: Opinion Ping-Pong and Birimo's pet monkey -> hamster are fun. seg0 "we suggested our own bottles" leaves out the action.
- B: The review steps (opinion with I think, reason with because, example with For example, I see your point, but, What do you mean by ~?) are right. contra = against and con = together are right. Respect's re is "back" in Latin, but "again" in the tipJa is fine for kids (single flag). In line 1, "because it's big" could mean the monkey, but in speech it clearly means the room (single flag, kept). The quiz option "B, I see your point, but I think." ends abruptly, but the slide shows the full line (kept).
- C: Same seg0 flag as A ("suggested our own bottles" stumbles). The rest reads well.
- Decision: seg0 -> "we suggested bringing our own bottles." (A+B).

#### L1044 Stage 4 Review: Explaining
- A: The bar graph, the report and Haru vs Riko about what helped are natural. "Our class" vs "the Children's Council" are both used, but "our class" is the pinned core (kept).
- B: The arithmetic is right (120 -> 40 is two thirds less). trans = across, e = out, pro = forward are right. The tip and the teacherScript use project as a verb (うつす / we project a picture).
- C: In seg10, the bare "Project," at the start of the sentence is a heteronym, and TTS reads the noun PROJ-ect. "Transport." alone is also a heteronym, but in seg9 the meaning line follows, so I kept it.
- Decision: seg10 "Project, throw a picture forward onto a screen." -> "To project is to throw a picture forward onto a screen." (B+C).

#### L1045 Stage 4 Review: Polite Requests
- A: The forty chairs in a circle, the checklist, "with an adult" for the banner and the ladder are good and safe. seg4 "Done? Tick!" is British. In the teacherScript, Riko asks Ms. Emma about the name cards, but in the conversation and seg7 Kiko asks.
- B: "Would you mind ~ing? - Not at all = OK" is explained clearly (seg6, seg19, the quiz), and "Would you mind not ~ing?" is a good extension. The story mismatch is the same as A's. The FOUR contrast review is right.
- C: In seg6, "Not at all!" has no speaker after "Haru asks softly, ..., Leo?". In seg7, "Sure, no problem." has no speaker after "Kiko asks me". "Let's meet at FOUR" in capitals is the house form for contrast stress (kept).
- Decision: teacherScript "Then Riko asks me" -> "Then Kiko asks me" (objective, story). seg4 "Tick!" -> "Check!" (objective, US policy). seg6 -> "Birimo says, Not at all!" with JA ビリモ「いいよ！」 (A+C). seg7 -> "I say, Sure, no problem." with JA わたし「…」 (A+C).

#### L1046 Two Sides of a Story
- A: The newsletter, Leo's ten-minute times tables and Priya's free evenings make a balanced pair. seg2 "A short writing about what you think" is not natural.
- B: The fact (it really happened) vs opinion check is right. "Somewhere in the middle" is explained by Kiko. The review set has all five kinds (ity, inter, contrast DOG, formal require, root spect), as the test needs. seg2 has the same flag as A. The teacherScript "popular plus ity" is a bare group.
- C: TTS misreads "ity" said alone. In seg9, "Popu-LAR-ity" is half a beat form.
- Decision: seg2 -> "A short piece of writing about what you think." (A+B). seg9 -> "Pop-u-LAR-i-ty!" (B+C). teacherScript -> "popular plus i, t, y." (B+C).

#### L1047 My Proposal Letter
- A: The mayor's note, the two benches and the compromise (two near the playground, two under the trees) are clear and match L1050. "Best wishes," is a soft letter ending. "Sincerely," is more formal, but it is fine for children (single flag). seg4 "Signature. Special writing at the end of a letter." does not say what a signature is.
- B: "Dear Mayor, I suggest that we + base verb" and "I look forward to hearing from you" are right. Not writing an address is good privacy teaching, and the goalJa keeps ほんとうの. In the seg8 JA, "じぶんの アドレスも かくの？" means an email address to a Japanese child, but the expression is じゅうしょ. "add ity" in seg9 and the teacherScript is a bare group.
- C: TTS misreads "ity" said alone. "KIKO feeds the fish" is fine.
- Decision: seg8 JA アドレス -> じゅうしょ (objective JA). "add ity" / "plus ity" -> "i, t, y" in seg9 (and JA) and the teacherScript (B+C). seg4 -> "Your name, signed at the end of a letter." with JA じぶんで かく なまえ (A+B).

#### L1048 Final Debate Practice
- A: The yes / no teams, the one-minute timer, "Sometimes you speak for a side you don't agree with" and the handshake are natural. "during summer vacation" is the block-wide fix (kept).
- B: First, I'd like to say that ~ / Second / That's true, but ~ / To sum up are right, and the quiz checks where To sum up goes. able -> ability (b, l, e -> b, i, l) is right. In conversation line 5, Kiko's "take turns during vacation" answers Riko and not Birimo's plant box. It is natural in a debate (single flag).
- C: In the teacherScript, "Ble changes to bil." is two bare groups. seg9 already spells them.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "B, l, e changes to b, i, l." (B+C).

#### L1049 Hall of Wisdom Review
- A: The memory board, "I reach up and take it down for him" and the old map to Deep Sea City are a good bridge to W36. Ms. Emma's own "Tomorrow is the big council meeting" is story time (kept).
- B: The memory board matches earlier lessons (fair, stand up for a friend, rights and responsibilities, visitor map, letter, less plastic). curious -> curiosity (o, u, s -> o, s) is right. costar and provide = give are fine. The story checks (map, sea) are present.
- C: "ous", "os" and "ity" are all said alone in seg9 and the teacherScript.
- Decision: seg9 and the teacherScript -> "o, u, s changes to o, s, plus / then add i, t, y" (B+C).

#### L1050 World 35 Grand Review
- A: The finale, Mayor Hart's red scarf, "four new benches", the council message and the bells are warm and consistent. seg20 has "Next, go and talk with Riko! Next is World thirty-six, the Deep Sea City!" That is two Nexts, and "the Deep Sea City" has an article, which no other lesson uses.
- B: Equality / international / THIS / children = kids (formal) / rupt are right. The 8 conversation lines, the speed TPR, the sixth leaf and the sea are all as the test needs.
- C: Same seg20 flag as A. "E-QUAL-i-ty" is in beat form and is fine.
- Decision: seg20 -> "After that, it's World thirty-six, Deep Sea City!" with JA その あとは (A+C). "Next, go and talk with Riko!" stays for the partner check.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Bare -ity and spelling-change groups in say / teacherScript**: "plus ity", "add ity", "ous changes to os" and "Ble changes to bil" appear in the W35 review lessons. Check L1021-1024 (the -ity block) and L1025 / L1030 / L1035 / L1040. Grep say and teacherScript for `\b(ity|ous|os|ble|bil)\b` and spell the letters.
- **Answers with no speaker after "X asks, ~?"**: L1041 seg8 and L1045 seg6 / seg7 needed a speaker. The same pattern ("Kiko asks, ...? It's ~.") is likely in other W35 Talk segments and in W36 Q&A lessons (L1071-1075). Grep say for `asks, [^?]*\? [A-Z]` with no "says" / "I answer" after it.
- **Heteronyms said alone in phonics lists**: "Project." / "Transport." / "Export." at the start of a sentence (L1042, L1044, L1045, L1049). I changed only the L1044 definition. "Your turn! Transport. Erupt. Project." still depends on TTS.
- **Bare "inter" / "co" said alone** ("inter means between", "co means together") were kept. TTS may read "co" as C-O. If the voice check shows it, use "c, o".
- **L1044's "For two months, Kiko and Riko counted"** together with L1042's Priya count is now consistent (31 a week, about 120 a month). If later lessons quote these numbers, keep "a week".
- Kept on purpose: the L1041 quiz answer slide that shows the fact/opinion learning line; "Best wishes," in the L1047 letter; "our class" in the L1044 core; "Let's meet at FOUR" / "DOG" / "KIKO" / "DRY" / "SATURDAY" / "THIS" in capitals; "Biscuit"; "Every voice matters!"; "Ding!"; "Hoo-hoo!"; "Well done!"; "Bravo!"; "Superb!"; story-internal "tonight" / "this morning" / "tomorrow".

### Run 9 review notes: L1051-1060 (world 36, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters, OK). There are 62 patches in 8 lessons. L1055 and L1060 have no change. There are **two learning-item changes**: (1) L1053 expression[1] "How far does sunlight reach? ― It reaches well only the top 200 meters." -> "... ― Bright sunlight reaches only the top 200 meters." ("well" sat between the verb and its object, like "speak well English"). The grammar field, aiTask target, recap, three slides, conversation line 3, seg6 and the depth-chart reading follow it. (2) L1058 expression[2] "... they go back down in the day." -> "... during the day." (the book page already says "During the day"). The aiTask target, recap, conversation line 4, seg7, seg17 and two slides follow it. No test or src line holds either string (I grepped tests/ and src/). The core expressions pinned by tests are untouched. The grammar order is clean: no new text adds the reason why / according to / in short / the main point is / in conclusion / let me explain / great question / look it up before its start lesson. Themes: (1) **Bare "tion" / "sion" in say** (L1057 seg10, L1058 seg10, L1059 seg10, and the same lines in the teacherScripts). These are now letters (t, i, o, n) or real words (information, conclusion). (2) **Answers with no speaker** (L1054 seg8 Kiko's "Ha ha, no!", L1057 seg7 Dr. Vale's "Of course!", L1059 seg7 Birimo's "No, it doesn't."). Without a name they sound like the asker or Ms. Emma answering. (3) **EN/JA mismatches** in L1056 (JA had extra lines in seg7, seg8 and seg20). (4) **Small fixes**: the L1051 "The person who drives it" had no antecedent; L1052 "Birimo blinks back" had the wrong story order; L1052 "where life lives"; L1053 "what you see" in the reason-why rule; L1057 "stress information on MA"; L1058 "pats a round tummy". All facts checked are right: no light below about 1,000 m; the sunlit zone is about 200 m; the midnight zone; deep water about 2-4 degrees; the Mariana Trench about 11,000 m; one atmosphere per 10 m and about 400 times at 4,000 m; soft bodies and no air sacs; big eyes; dumbo octopus fins like ears; giant squid eyes about 27 cm; tube worms at vents hotter than 350 degrees; vampire squid eating marine snow; lanternfish huge in number and migrating up at night and down by day; the anglerfish lure ("some anglerfish"). Safety is also right: search online with a grown-up, never click prize pop-ups, ask who wrote a website. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 220-238 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1051 Down to Deep Sea City
- A: The research harbor, the silver diving watch, "I'm the pilot, so you're safe with me" and the welcome sign are warm and natural. In seg4, "Pilot. The person who drives it." has no "it" to point to (the segment before was about a porthole on a ship).
- B: "This is the place where + sentence" is clear. The fact "below about 1,000 meters, it is completely dark" is right. Bio = life, biology / biography / biome are right. The seg4 "it" flag is the same as A's.
- C: Reads well. "Bio. Say bye-oh." is two real words, so TTS reads it right (kept). The numbers in the sign are digits inside the teacherScript reading, which is fine.
- Decision: seg4 (say, subtitleEn) -> "The person who drives the submarine." with JA せんすいていを うごかす 人 (A+B).

#### L1052 Creatures That Glow
- A: The dark aquarium hall, "Please do not tap on the glass" and Birimo's "Blink, blink! That means hello!" are fun. In seg10, "Every place on Earth where life lives" sounds odd.
- B: The reasons to glow (find food, hide, talk, scare enemies) and "to + verb" for why are right. "Some anglerfish have a glowing lure" hedges well (in most species only females have it). In seg8, "Birimo blinks back at Lumi" reverses the story: in the conversation Birimo blinks first and Lumi blinks back.
- C: The seg10 "where life lives" echo is awkward to say. "Then I'll blink my hands" (conversation) is playful Birimo talk (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg8 -> "Birimo blinks his fingers at Lumi." (objective, story order). seg10 -> "Every place on Earth where there is life." (A+C).

#### L1053 The Reason Why It's Dark
- A: "Did somebody turn off the sea?" is a great hook. The expression "It reaches well only the top 200 meters" is not something a native speaker says. The chart line "Sunlight reaches well only this top part" has the same problem.
- B: "The reason why ~ is that ~" (not "is because") is modeled correctly everywhere, and it first appears here as the order needs. "well" between the verb and its object is a word-order error. The fact needs "bright", because dim light goes down to 1,000 m. In seg8, the rule "The reason why, then what you see" is wrong: the slot is the fact being explained (the example is "why I'm cold"). "About 2 to 4 degrees" with no Celsius may confuse a US reader, but L1072 has the same wording in a learning item, so it is a block-wide note.
- C: "reaches well only the top" is choppy to say. The numbers are words in say.
- Decision: expression[1] and every copy -> "Bright sunlight reaches only the top 200 meters." (grammar field, aiTask target, recap, seg6/15/20 slides, conversation line 3, seg6, the chart and its question; JA 明るい 日の光が とどくのは) (A+B, learning item). seg8 "what you see" -> "what happens" with JA おきて いる こと (A+B).

#### L1054 Life Under Pressure
- A: Pushing your hands together for pressure, "Do they wear tiny metal suits?" and the dumbo octopus are fun. In seg8, "Ha ha, no!" has no speaker, so it sounds like Ms. Emma laughing at Birimo.
- B: The facts are right (one atmosphere per 10 m, about 400 times at 4,000 m, soft bodies, no air sacs, big eyes). The rule "Because + reason" is clear. "very, very strong" (Dr. Vale) is a keeper.
- C: Same seg8 flag: the voice cannot show that Kiko is answering.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Kiko laughs. Ha ha, no! ..." with JA キコちゃんが わらうよ。「あはは、ちがうよ！」 (A+C).

#### L1055 Deep Sea Wonder Talk
- A: The Wonder Talk cards, "Where did you find that?" as the house rule, and the first pearl work well. The teacherScript says "Dr. Vale, Lumi and I sit in the front row", though Lumi swims in water tunnels (single flag, kept).
- B: The facts are right (giant squid eyes about 27 cm, dinner plates; tube worms at vents hotter than 350 degrees, with no sunlight; vampire squid eat marine snow, not blood). The pearl appears in story and conversation as the test needs. "One amazing fact is that ~ / Another ~" is clear.
- C: Reads well. "Plate eyes! Card up!" is a good speed round.
- Decision: no change.

#### L1056 My Research Question
- A: The Research Center, the whiteboard steps and Birimo's "sleepy" question are natural. "Scientists are still finding out!" is a good honest answer.
- B: The stress claims are right for US English (re-SEARCH is always fine, and some people say RE-search for the noun; com-PARE, re-SULT, SUM-ma-ry). The JA has lines with no English: seg7 has Birimo's "まだ だれも しらないの？ じゃあ しらべて みよう！", seg8 has "まだ だれも しらなくても だいじょうぶ。だから けんきゅう するんだよ！", and seg20 has "つよい ところで 手も たたけるね。"
- C: Reads well. The capital syllables (SEARCH, PARE, SULT, SUM) are the house beat form.
- Decision: seg8 EN adds "If nobody knows yet, that's OK. That's why we do research!" (from the whiteboard; it matches the JA and the slide's "Nobody knows yet?"). The extra JA in seg7 and seg20 is dropped (objective JA).

#### L1057 Where Can We Find Information?
- A: Mr. Oswin's slow "Wel... come..." and his poster ("anyone can make a website, so always ask who wrote it", "Search online with a grown-up") are good. In seg7, "Can I interview you for my research? Of course!" gives the answer no speaker. In seg19, "stress information on MA" is teacher jargon.
- B: The stress words are right (EV-i-dence, dis-COV-er, ex-PLAIN, in-for-MA-tion, and the -tion rule). "I see your point, but" is a W35 review form and fine. The expression "Try the Coral Library" while they are already in it is a small oddity (single flag, kept).
- C: The seg7 speaker flag is the same. In seg10 and the teacherScript, the bare "tion" is read as "tie-on". In seg19, the bare "MA" may be spelled out.
- Decision: seg7 -> "Dr. Vale says, Of course! ..." with JA ヴェイルはかせ「…」 (A+C). seg10 and teacherScript -> "Words that end in t, i, o, n are strong just before the ending." (B+C). seg19 -> "and clap the strong beat in information." with JA to match (A+C).

#### L1058 According to the Book
- A: Lights of the Deep, Chapter 3, Lumi's cousins and "According to my tummy" are fun and natural. The expression "they go back down in the day" is not how a US speaker says it ("during the day"). In seg7, "Birimo pats a round tummy" sounds like someone else's tummy.
- B: "According to this book, ~" first appears here as the order needs. The lanternfish migration is right. "In the day" is the same flag as A's, and the book page itself says "During the day". In seg10, "Sion is like tion" uses bare letter groups.
- C: TTS misreads "Sion" and "tion". "Ex, ob." in seg9 is pronounceable (single flag, kept). The seg7 "a round tummy" flag is the same as A's.
- Decision: expression[2] and every copy -> "during the day", and seg17 -> "During the day, back down." (A+B, learning item). seg7 -> "pats his round tummy" (A+C). seg10 -> "Conclusion ends like information. The beat just before the ending is strong." and teacherScript -> "Words like information and conclusion are strong just before the ending." (B+C).

#### L1059 Is This Website Reliable?
- A: The "Fishy Fun Facts Page" (no author, no date, "Click here to win a free submarine!") is a very good model. The "never click on prizes" line and the Mr. Oswin check are good. In seg7, "Does it show its sources? No, it doesn't." gives the answer no speaker.
- B: "Who wrote it? When was it written?" (passive) is clear. "I don't think so, because ~" is right. The Japanese hint (しんらい できる) is allowed here. The stress words are right (pres-en-TA-tion, im-POR-tant, TOP-ic, con-CLUDE).
- C: The seg7 speaker flag is the same. In seg10 and the teacherScript, "ends in tion" is a bare group.
- Decision: seg7 -> "Birimo says, No, it doesn't." with JA ビリモ「…」 (A+C). seg10 and teacherScript -> "ends in t, i, o, n" (B+C).

#### L1060 Research Plan
- A: The round table, the plan card (with "with Ms. Emma", which is the children's own writing and stays) and Birimo wanting to interview a giant squid are natural. Riko's "After that, we'll check websites" follows Haru's two steps, so it fits the card's order.
- B: "First, we'll ~. Then, we'll ~. Finally, we'll ~." and "It will take about three weeks" are right. "Three weeks" fits after L1051/L1056 said "in one month". The second pearl is in story and conversation.
- C: Reads well. The review of re-SEARCH / re-SULT / bio is short and clear.
- Decision: no change.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Bare "tion" / "sion" in say**: L1057-1059 had them. Expect the same in L1061-1064 (pause lessons), L1066-1069 ("ology" / "ologist" alone: TTS may read "ology" oddly), L1071-1074 ("°C", "−2"), and the reviews L1075-1080. Grep say for `\b(tion|sion|ology|ologist|bio|ex|ob)\b` and spell the letters or use a real word.
- **Answers with no speaker after "X asks, ~?"**: three cases here. Grep say for `asks, [^.?!]*\? (No|Yes|Of course|Sure)` in L1061-1080.
- **"degrees" with no Celsius**: L1053 (and the conversation), L1055 ("hotter than 350 degrees") and the L1072 learning expression "about 2 to 4 degrees" never say Celsius. A US child reads degrees as Fahrenheit (2-4 °F would be frozen). I did not change it here, because L1072 owns the units lesson and its expression. Consider "degrees Celsius" at the first mention in each lesson (L1053, L1055, L1072-1074), and keep the bare form after that.
- **"reaches well"**: L1071 seg10 "Two hundred meters, where sunlight reaches well." is fine grammar (no object), but it may want "bright sunlight" to match the new L1053 wording.
- Kept on purpose: "Bio. Say bye-oh." (real words); "Then I'll blink my hands." (Birimo); "Lumi and I sit in the front row" (L1055); "Try the Coral Library" while in it (L1057); "Ex, ob." (L1058); Kiko's plan card "with Ms. Emma"; Mr. Oswin's "Wel... come..." and "Check... your... sources!"; "very, very strong" (Dr. Vale); all "Well done!" / "Thumbs up!" items.

### Run 9 review notes: L1061-1070 (world 36, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters, OK). There are 54 patches, and every lesson has at least one. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean ("in short" starts in L1061, "the main point is" in L1062, "in conclusion" in L1069, "according to" is used only after L1058), and no "let me explain" / "great question" / "look it up" appears before L1071. Themes: (1) **Syllables said one by one in say** (most of the patches): the -ology / -ologist lessons (L1066-1070) and the L1065 review spelled the beats as separate bare pieces ("bi, OL, o, gist", "zo, OL, o, gy", "e, COL, o, gy", "ar, chae, OL, o, gy", "me, te, or, OL, o, gy", "dis, COV, er", "ex, PER, i, ment"). TTS reads "i" as eye, "bi" as bye and "o" as oh, and garbles "gy" and "chae". They now use the hyphen beat form that L1058 already uses ("Ex-PER-i-ment"), with the subtitleJa to match. (2) **Facts**: L1067 said "Deep water is only about 2 to 4 degrees". A US child reads that as Fahrenheit, which is below freezing, so it now says Celsius (in the reading and in the answer). L1070 called the deep sea "a big area with its own climate, plants and animals". There are no plants in the dark deep sea (L1063 says so), so it now says "special conditions and living things". (3) **Speakers and wording**: the L1067 seg8 answer had no speaker after "Riko asks", and L1064 seg7 "Birimo flashes." came before a quote with no "says". L1061 "never stop inside the deep sea" and L1063 "don't stop inside swim up to find" were confusing when read aloud. L1067 and L1068 had the awkward "Sunlight reaches well only the top part of the ocean". L1065 said "remembers his source:" and then gave a fact. L1062 had "a fun detail, but it's a detail", "Details help it" and "The ears". L1066 was missing a period ("right here At the festival"). All facts checked are right: anglerfish lure (said as "some anglerfish"), plastic in the deepest trench at about 11,000 m, dumbo octopus ear fins, marine snow (dead plants, dead animals and poop; the vampire squid eats it), lanternfish going up at night and down by day, four reasons for bioluminescence, giant squid 12-13 m with dinner-plate eyes, sunlight to about 200 m and none below about 1,000 m (midnight zone), Deep Sea City at 3,800 m, and one atmosphere per 10 m (about 400 times at 4,000 m). The pearls are in the right places (third in L1065, fourth in L1070). The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 207-224 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1061 In Short
- A: Mr. Oswin's thick book, Riko's five-minute page and Haru's "anglerfish are very patient" are warm and natural. "Sum up your favorite animal. In short, cats love to sleep in the sun." is a good Your turn. In seg10, "But never stop inside the deep sea!" sounds like a rule about the place, not the words.
- B: In short = keep the big idea and cut the details is taught well, and it first appears here as the order needs. The pause rules (after an opener, at a comma, after a long subject, never inside a group) are right. The anglerfish facts are hedged correctly ("Some anglerfish have a glowing lure"). In conversation line 8, the JA "オズウィンさんの 研究センターの 本" can be read as "Mr. Oswin's research center". That is a small ambiguity (single flag, kept).
- C: "pause here" inside the model sentences can be performed. The seg10 "inside the deep sea" is the same flag as A. Also, seg10's model sentence has no "the deep sea" in it.
- Decision: in seg10 (say, subtitleEn), "never stop inside the deep sea" -> "never pause in the middle of a group like the deep sea" (the teacherScript wording), with JA to match (A+C).

#### L1062 The Main Point Is…
- A: Dr. Vale's article, Birimo's "it's my favorite detail!" and the plastic fact are good. In the teacherScript, "It's a fun detail, but it's a detail." repeats itself. In seg8, "Details help it" is vague.
- B: Main point vs detail is clear, and the headline tip is right. The facts are right (plastic in the deepest trench at about 11,000 m; octopuses with ear-like fins). The seg8 flag is the same as A's: the teacherScript teaches "Details support the main point". In seg19, "The ears are a fun detail" is wrong, because they are fins.
- C: Same flag on the teacherScript line (it has no contrast to stress). "The main point is, pause here, that" is fine for a presenter.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "It's a fun fact, but it's a detail." (A+C). seg8 -> "Details support it" (A+B). seg19 -> "The ear fins are a fun detail." with JA 耳みたいな ひれは (A+B). The lowercase quiz options ("A, the deep sea...") are a single flag, kept.

#### L1063 Summarize in Three Sentences
- A: Birimo with his nose on the glass ("Is it snowing? Under the sea?") and "A vampire that eats snow?" are fun. The about / says / shows frame is clear.
- B: Marine snow is right (dead plants, dead animals, poop; there are no plants down there; the vampire squid eats it). The lanternfish facts in phonics are right (up at night, down by day). In seg10, "don't stop inside swim up to find" is hard to parse.
- C: Same seg10 flag: read aloud, "inside swim up to find" sounds like a broken sentence.
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "don't stop in the middle of swim up to find" (A+C). The JA (とちゅうでは) already says this.

#### L1064 Notes, Not Sentences
- A: Kiko writing every word and falling three sentences behind is very real. The toast / shoes / bus Your turn is nice. In seg7, "Birimo flashes. Some animals flash..." gives the quote no speaker.
- B: The four reasons for bioluminescence (find food, hide, talk, scare enemies) are right and match L1069. "Take notes: glow, food, dark." is a label more than a sentence, but it is the pinned learning item (kept). Riko asking and answering her own question in conversation line 1 is fine as a model.
- C: Same seg7 flag. Birimo's feminine JA (いるのよ) when he plays Dr. Vale is kept, as in earlier blocks.
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Birimo flashes and says, Some animals..." (A+C).

#### L1065 Summary Challenge
- A: The sand timer, "nobody laughs at mistakes" and Birimo's "In short, it's a mystery!" are great. In the teacherScript, "He even remembers his source: the biggest ones measured are..." is followed by a fact, not a source.
- B: The giant squid fact is right (12-13 m). Short / clear / true and "say where you found your facts" are good habits. The source flag is the same as A's (the conversation says "According to Mr. Oswin's book"). In seg9, "ex, PER, i, ment" breaks the word into bare pieces.
- C: In seg9, TTS reads "i" as eye. L1058 already uses "Ex-PER-i-ment". "Hi, hi, everyone!" is playful (single flag, kept).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "He even gives his source. According to Mr. Oswin's book, ..." (A+B). seg9 -> "Ex-PER-i-ment" in EN and JA (B+C).

#### L1066 Today, I'd Like to Talk About…
- A: The Great Dome, Haru's gulp, and "Good morning, fish and friends!" are good. "Good morning, everyone" is right here as a presenter's opening (block-wide keeper). In the teacherScript, "right here At the festival" is missing a period.
- B: The hello -> question -> topic order is clear. -ology = study of and -ologist = person who studies are right, and bi-OL-o-gist and zo-OL-o-gy are right. The facts are right (no sunlight below about 1,000 m; dinner-plate squid eyes). In seg9 and seg10, the syllables are said as separate bare pieces.
- C: TTS reads "bi" as bye and "o" as oh, and "gy" is unreadable. The missing period runs the two sentences together.
- Decision: teacherScript period (objective). seg9 -> "Bi-OL-o-gist!" and seg10 -> "Zo-OL-o-gy." in EN and JA (B+C). seg7 JA ディナーの おさら vs 夕ごはんの おさら is a single flag, kept.

#### L1067 First, Second, Finally
- A: Twenty mixed-up facts, the note cards and "signs on a path" are good. In the reading, "Sunlight reaches well only the top part of the ocean, about 200 meters." is awkward. In seg8, the answer after "Riko asks" has no speaker.
- B: Signposting with first / second / finally is clear. The pressure facts are right (one atmosphere per 10 m, about 400 times at 4,000 m), and so is the jelly-like-bodies point. "About 2 to 4 degrees" is objectively misleading for a US child, who reads it as Fahrenheit. "Eco means home" and "Techno means skill" are fine.
- C: The "reaches well only" line is hard to say. Same seg8 flag. In seg9 and seg10, "e, COL, o, gy" and "tech, NOL, o, gy" are bare pieces.
- Decision: reading -> "Sunlight only lights up the top part of the ocean, about the first 200 meters." (A+C). "2 to 4 degrees" -> "2 to 4 degrees Celsius" in the reading and the answer (objective fact). seg8 -> "Haru says, According to Dr. Vale, ..." with JA ハルくん「…」 (A+C). seg9 / seg10 -> "E-COL-o-gy!" and "Tech-NOL-o-gy." (B+C).

#### L1068 Look at This Picture
- A: Birimo in the back row ("I can't see the bottom of the graph!") and stepping to the side are very practical. The reading has the same "sunlight reaches well only" line as L1067, and the question "How deep does sunlight reach well?" is unnatural.
- B: The light graph is right (bright near the surface, falling fast after about 200 m, zero below about 1,000 m = midnight zone, Deep Sea City at 3,800 m). archaeology is an accepted US spelling and is a phonics table key (kept). ar-chae-OL-o-gy and my-THOL-o-gy are right.
- C: Same reading flag. In seg9 and seg10, "ar, chae, OL, o, gy" and "my, THOL, o, gy" are bare pieces ("chae" is unreadable alone). "Hello, brilliant researchers!" uses brilliant as an adjective, which is fine in US English (single flag, kept).
- Decision: reading -> "But sunlight only lights up the top part of the ocean, about the first 200 meters." and question -> "How deep does bright sunlight go?" (A+C). seg9 / seg10 -> "Ar-chae-OL-o-gy!" and "My-THOL-o-gy." (B+C).

#### L1069 In Conclusion
- A: Kiko's "That's all. Bye!" and Lumi's "That ending gave me goosebumps, and I'm a fish!" are great. The volcanologist link to Dr. Mori (W33) is a nice callback.
- B: Conclusion = main point again plus a message is clear, and "in conclusion" first appears here. Kiko's ending matches the four reasons from L1064. "Much of the deep sea has never been explored" is right. me-te-or-OL-o-gy and vol-can-OL-o-gist are right.
- C: In seg9 and seg10, "me, te, or, OL, o, gy" and "vol, can, OL, o, gist" are six and five bare pieces in a row.
- Decision: seg9 -> "Me-te-or-OL-o-gy!" and seg10 -> "Vol-can-OL-o-gist." in EN and JA (B+C).

#### L1070 Practice Presentation
- A: Mr. Oswin's "Even a turtle can win a race if he takes his time" and Birimo playing Oswin ("Slow and steady!") are lovely. The rehearsal card tips are real presenter advice. "Something good, then one helpful idea" is a good feedback model.
- B: The fourth pearl is in the right place. The teacherScript calls the deep sea "a big area with its own climate, plants and animals". That is objectively wrong for the dark deep sea, and it contradicts L1063 ("there are no plants"). seg9 says "similar weather and living things" for biomes in general, which is fine. dis-COV-er is right.
- C: "Firstthedeepseaisdarksecondit..." is meant to sound rushed (single flag, kept). In seg9 and seg10, "bi, OL, o, gist" and "dis, COV, er" are bare pieces.
- Decision: teacherScript biome line -> "a big area with its own special conditions and living things" (objective fact). seg9 -> "Bi-OL-o-gist." and seg10 -> "Dis-COV-er." in EN and JA (B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Syllables said one by one in say** ("x, YY, z"): the W36 stress and -ology lessons write the beats as comma-separated bare pieces. Grep say / subtitleEn in L1051-1080 for `\b[a-z]{1,5}, [A-Z]{2,5}, ` and change them to the hyphen beat form ("Bi-OL-o-gist"), with subtitleJa to match. L1071-1074 (numbers) and the reviews L1075 and L1080 may have the same pattern.
- **src/lib/curriculum/w36Phonics.ts line 31** (biome definition): "a large area with its own climate, plants and animals, like a desert or the deep sea" has the same fact problem (no plants in the deep sea). I did not edit src. Suggested fix: "with its own climate and living things", or drop "or the deep sea".
- **Bare "degrees"**: any W36 line with a water temperature (L1071-1074 numbers and units, L1075, L1080) should say "degrees Celsius" at least once, so a US child does not hear Fahrenheit.
- **"Sunlight reaches well only the top part"**: this was in both L1067 and L1068. If it is copied in L1075 or L1080, use "Sunlight only lights up the top part of the ocean, about the first 200 meters."
- Kept on purpose: "Take notes: glow, food, dark." (pinned item); "Good morning, everyone." as a presenter opening (L1066); "Hello, brilliant researchers!" (adjective); "Hi, hi, everyone!"; "Firstthedeepseaisdarksecondit..."; archaeology spelling (table key); Birimo's よ / のよ / じゃ endings when he plays a character; "Very, very small piece" in the L1063 definition; Mr. Oswin's normal-speed lines in L1061 (not every line needs the slow style); the lowercase quiz options.

### Run 9 review notes: L1071-1080 (world 36, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 54 patches. L1077 has no change. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "great question" / "let me explain" first appear in L1071 and "look it up" in L1074, and no new text moves them earlier. Themes: (1) **Speaker changes with no names in say**: when one segment has two or three speakers, the voice cannot show who is talking. Answers sound like the asker answering herself (L1072 seg8, L1073 seg7-8, L1074 seg8, L1075 seg6-7, L1076 seg7, L1078 seg8, L1080 seg7; also the L1071 teacherScript). I added "Haru says," / "Riko asks me," / "I say," and matched the JA. (2) **Bare letter groups in stress tips**: "The strong beat is on OL" (L1075 ecology, L1079 technology) names a group that is not a syllable, and TTS misreads it. It is now COL / NOL, as L1070 has "a strong COL". "just before tion" (L1078 seg10 and teacherScript) is now "the ending". (3) **Objective errors**: L1072 "Because sunlight can't reach this deep, so nothing warms the water" (because + so); the L1072 degree rule "two or more / bigger than 1 takes s" sits next to "minus two degrees" (seg10 and tipJa); L1080 has Riko first in the teacherScript and program, but Haru first in the conversation and seg6; JA ミズ・エマ (L1072 conversation, L1074 topic) is now エマ先生; L1075 「はい、おねがい！」 for "Me, please!"; L1078 two とっても for three verys. (4) **Small fixes**: L1072 quiz had no question; L1076 "Two friends" in a sources frame; L1079 "sum up in short"; L1080 "checked ... checked"; L1071 "reach well?". All facts checked are right: sunlight to about 200 m and darkness below about 1,000 m; 3,800 m; Mariana Trench about 11,000 m / 11 km; deep water about 2-4 °C and polar seawater down to about −2 °C; vents hotter than 350 °C with tube worms; giant squid eyes about 27 cm, females bigger, the biggest measured about 12-13 m (source C: most big ones 10-13 m); about 400 times the surface pressure at 4,000 m; lanternfish common and migrating up at night to feed; plastic found in the deepest trench. The L1078 model script is 117 words ("about 120 words" is fine). The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 213-234 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1071 That's a Great Question
- A: The card "How to Answer a Question" and "a question is a gift" are warm and natural. In the teacherScript, "Birimo raises his hand right away. Kiko, why is it so dark down here?" has no speaker, so it sounds like Ms. Emma asking Kiko. "How deep does sunlight reach well?" is odd.
- B: The facts are right (200 m, 1,000 m, 3,800 m, about 11,000 m for the Mariana Trench). The number reading (the number left of the comma = thousands) is clear. The teacherScript says Dr. Vale opens question time and the conversation says Ms. Emma. Both are fine in their own telling (single flag, kept).
- C: The same two teacherScript flags. The seg 9-10 numbers are written as words, which is good for TTS.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "raises his hand right away and asks, Kiko, ..." and "How deep does sunlight reach?" (A+C).

#### L1072 I'm Not Sure, But…
- A: Riko's honest guess and Dr. Vale's notebook page are good models. The seg18 quiz gives options but no question ("What do you do?"). The JA has いちばん いいのは どれ？ and 大きな かず, which are not in the EN.
- B: Conversation line 4, "Because sunlight can't reach this deep, so nothing warms the water", is a because + so error. The degree rule in seg10 ("Two or more? Degrees") and in tipJa ("1 より 大きい ときは") sits right next to "minus two degrees", which also takes s. Only one degree has no s. The JA ミズ・エマ breaks block policy. The facts are right (2-4 °C, −2 °C near the Poles, vents over 350 °C, tube worms).
- C: seg8 runs Birimo, Riko and Ms. Emma together with no names. The JA names only Birimo.
- Decision: line 4 -> "Because sunlight can't reach this deep, nothing warms the water." (objective). Line 4 JA -> エマ先生 (objective JA). seg8 adds "Birimo says," / "Riko asks me," / "I say," with matching JA (A+C). seg18 adds "What do you do?" with matching JA (A+B). seg10 -> "Just one? Degree. Any other number? Degrees, with an s!" with matching JA, and tipJa -> "1 ど の ときだけ degree、ほかは degrees と s が つくよ（minus two degrees も）" (objective). "°C" is read as just "degrees", not "degrees Celsius". That is a fine simplification (single flag, kept).

#### L1073 Asking Good Questions
- A: The question guide (topic first, one question, follow-up) is natural classroom English. In seg7, "That's a great question..." comes right after Kiko's question with no speaker. In seg8, "Females are bigger!" comes right after "Riko asks".
- B: The facts are right (eyes about 27 cm, females bigger, 12-13 m, 11 km). "Which are bigger, males or females?" is correct. "The biggest giant squid measured are about thirteen meters long" (seg10) is fine as a plural.
- C: The same seg7 and seg8 speaker flags. "Thir-TEEN" works well.
- Decision: seg7 -> "Haru says, That's a great question..." and seg8 -> "Haru says, Females are bigger!", with JA ハルくん (A+C).

#### L1074 I'll Look It Up
- A: The promise and keeping it, plus Mr. Oswin's "Check your sources!", are good. In seg8, "I looked it up! Really? What did you find out? Many lanternfish..." has two speakers with no names.
- B: The facts are right (about 400 times the pressure at 4,000 m, 3.8 km = Deep Sea City, lanternfish rising at night, sleep still unknown). The point = point and per = for each explanations are clear. Conversation line 6 says "What else did you find out?" instead of the target. That fits after the first fact (kept). The school.topic JA ミズ・エマ breaks block policy.
- C: The same seg8 flag.
- Decision: seg8 adds "Haru asks," and "Birimo says," with JA names (A+C). topic -> エマ先生と (objective JA).

#### L1075 Q&A Session
- A: The host card (thank the speaker, choose, "You first, and then you", two minutes left) is natural. seg6 has Riko's host line unnamed. seg7 has three speakers (host, asker, speaker) with no names.
- B: The host language is right. In seg10, "The strong beat is on OL" names letters that are not a syllable of e-COL-o-gy. The seg8 JA 「はい、おねがい！」 drops "Me".
- C: seg6 and seg7 cannot be voiced clearly. TTS spells "OL" or reads it as "ol".
- Decision: seg6 adds "Riko says," and seg7 adds "Riko says," / "Kiko says," / "Haru says," with JA names (A+C). seg10 -> "on COL" with JA (B+C). seg8 JA -> 「はい、ぼく！」 (objective JA). The "pause here" style in seg11 is kept (block house style, see below).

#### L1076 Reading Three Sources
- A: The three sources, "Maybe all of them!" and "Sources can disagree. That's normal" are a good lesson. seg17 sets up "Two friends tell you different things" but asks for "Source A says...".
- B: The facts in the three sources agree with L1073 and L1080. "Female. Like a mother animal." is a loose but common kid gloss (single flag, kept). The friends vs sources mismatch is the same flag as A's.
- C: In seg7, the answer to "Kiko asks, What does source C say?" has no speaker.
- Decision: seg7 adds "Haru says," with JA (A+C). seg17 -> "Two books tell you different things." with JA 2 さつの 本が (A+B).

#### L1077 Protecting the Deep Sea
- A: The plastic bag photo, the poster's three actions and "Small actions can make a big difference!" are natural and age-right.
- B: The facts are right (plastic found in the deepest trench, much of the deep sea unexplored). "the places where" is used correctly. The safety and the tone are fine.
- C: Reads well. seg7's unnamed "Plastic can travel a long way in the ocean." works as Ms. Emma's own narration, and the conversation gives it to her.
- Decision: no change.

#### L1078 My Presentation Script
- A: The model script with First / Second / Finally and a source is a strong model, and Birimo's "very, very, very cute" is the intended bad draft. In seg8, "Where did you find that? I found it..." has no speakers.
- B: "Today, I'd like to talk about ~ / In conclusion, ~" is taught correctly. The reading is 117 words, which fits "about 120 words". The teacherScript line "Presentation is strong just before -tion" is unnatural. The seg19 JA has two とっても for three verys.
- C: The seg8 speaker flag. "Clap the beat just before tion" has a bare "tion" that TTS misreads.
- Decision: seg8 adds "Riko asks," / "Haru says," with JA names (A+C). seg10 -> "just before the ending" (B+C). teacherScript -> "Presentation has a strong beat just before the ending, pres-en-TA-tion." (B+C). seg19 JA gets three とっても (objective JA). The "very, very, very" learning-line flag is kept on purpose (it is the bad example the lesson fixes).

#### L1079 Deep Sea City Review
- A: The goodbye tour, Lumi's card and the carved, locked door are a good build-up. seg20 "You can sum up in short" reads as an idiom, not as the phrase.
- B: The review content is right (sym = together, bio = life, SUM-ma-ry). In seg10, "The beat is on OL" has the same non-syllable problem as L1075. Technology is not "study of" like the other -ology words, but the lesson does not claim that (kept).
- C: TTS misreads "OL".
- Decision: seg10 -> "The strong beat is on NOL." with JA (B+C). seg20 -> "sum up with In short" (A+B).

#### L1080 World 36 Grand Review
- A: The festival, the sixth pearl and the opening door are a satisfying close. The program sentence "checked websites, asked experts and checked their sources" repeats checked. In seg7, "As big as dinner plates!" has no speaker.
- B: The story contradicts itself: the teacherScript and program list Riko first, but the conversation ("Haru, you're first.") and seg6 ("Haru is first.") start with Haru. The facts are consistent with L1073 and L1076. The re-SEARCH claim matches the W36 phonics.
- C: The seg7 speaker flag and the checked / checked rhythm.
- Decision: teacherScript, two sentences reordered so that Haru comes first (objective story). "checked websites" -> "looked at websites" (A+C). seg7 -> "Haru answers, As big as dinner plates!" with JA (A+C). The speed TPR and the pearl / door story checks are untouched.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Speaker changes inside one say**: the most common issue in this group. A segment that quotes two or three characters with only the first one named makes the answer sound like the asker. Check L1051-1070 for "X asks, Q? A." and "Q? A." patterns (grep say for `\? [A-Z][^?]*\.$` after "asks,") and add "Y says," with JA names.
- **"pause here" inside model sentences** (L1061-1080, e.g. "The main point is, pause here, that ..."): the voice reads the words "pause here", and a child copying after "Your turn!" may repeat them. This is house style in the whole block, so I kept it. A block-wide fix could say "Pause after X." first and then give the clean sentence once. Single owner decision.
- **Stress tips naming letter groups**: "on OL" (L1075, L1079), "just before tion" (L1078, and L1058 seg10 "strong just before tion"). Use the real stressed syllable (COL / NOL) or "the ending". The tipJa uses of "OL を つよく" are written, not spoken, so I kept them.
- **JA ミズ・エマ**: only L1072 and L1074 had it in W36. Both are fixed.
- Kept on purpose: Mr. Oswin's slow speech, "very, very slowly" narration, Birimo's "very, very, very cute" bad draft, "Bravo, researchers!", "Thumbs-up", "Let's find out!", the teacherScript Dr. Vale vs conversation Ms. Emma opening question time (L1071), Lumi vs Ms. Emma asking "What did you learn here?" (L1079), °C read as just "degrees", and the comma in "I loved the aquarium hall, because ..." (L1079, single flag).

| 1023 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Should students wear uniforms? Today, Olive rings her bell, and Amara raises her hand. She says, At my school, break time is only ten minutes. That's not enough time to go outside and come back! Olive says, Thank you, Amara. Who has an opinion? Remember to give a reason. Haru raises his hand and says, I think we should have longer breaks because we need time to relax. Say it with me. I think we should have longer breaks because we need time to relax. Excellent! An opinion is stronger with a reason and an example. Haru adds, For example, we can play outside and talk with our friends. Then Priya says, I partly agree. But then school will finish later. Both ideas are fair. Olive gives everyone a short article from the Hall's newsletter. Let's read the article. "Do children need longer breaks? Some people say yes. After a good break, many children can concentrate better in class. Break time is also an important activity, because children make friends, solve small problems, and move their bodies.
